across all conditions.\nAll experiments use the HuggingFace TRL \\texttt{SFTTrainer} with LoRA \\citep{hu2022lora} applied to all linear layers.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters (shared across all conditions).}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Hyperparameter} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA rank ($r$)            & 16 \\\\\nLoRA alpha ($\\alpha$)      & 32 \\\\\nLoRA dropout               & 0.05 \\\\\nLoRA target modules        & all linear layers \\\\\n\\midrule\nEpochs                     & 3 \\\\\nPer-device batch size       & 4 \\\\\nGradient accumulation steps & 8 \\\\\nEffective batch size        & 32 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLearning rate               & $2 \\times 10^{-4}$ \\\\\nLR scheduler                & linear decay \\\\\nWarmup ratio                & 0.1 \\\\\nWeight decay                & 0.01 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMax sequence length         & 2048 tokens \\\\\nPrimary seed                & 42 \\\\\nRobustness seeds            & 123, 456 \\\\\n\\midrule\nHardware                    & 1$\\times$ RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell (94\\,GB VRAM) \\\\\nFramework                   & HuggingFace TRL SFTTrainer \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}%\n}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:train_time} reports per-condition training wall-clock times.\nConditions with longer trace inputs (e.g., \\texttt{full\\_trace}, VOTA) require proportionally more training time due to increased sequence lengths.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training wall-clock time per condition on a single RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPU. Where multiple seeds were run, individual times are listed.}\n\\label{tab:train_time}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{llr}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed(s)} & \\textbf{Time (h)} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{no\\_trace}       & 42         & ${\\sim}$0.5 \\\\\n\\texttt{full\\_trace}     & 42         & ${\\sim}$2.2 \\\\\n\\texttt{label\\_only}     & 42         & ${\\sim}$2.2 \\\\\nVOTA                     & 42         & ${\\sim}$2.3 \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 42         & ${\\sim}$2.1 \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 123        & ${\\sim}$2.1 \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 456        & ${\\sim}$1.3 \\\\\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}        & 42         & ${\\sim}$1.8 \\\\\n\\texttt{loop\\_only}      & 42         & ${\\sim}$2.1 \\\\\n\\texttt{VOTA\\_no\\_ae}    & 42         & ${\\sim}$1.8 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{VOTA Trace Examples}\n\\label{sec:vota_examples}\n\nWe provide two representative VOTA trace examples illustrating the fail and pass cases.\nIn the fail case, the exception event provides an unambiguous fault signal.\nIn the pass case, the absence of an exception event combined with a matching assertion point confirms correct execution.\n\n\\paragraph{Fail example.}\nA \\texttt{ZeroDivisionError} triggers an \\texttt{[ERROR]} event, providing a direct fault signal that is sufficient for the model to predict \\texttt{fail}.\n\n\\begin{small}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n## Function\ndef divide(a, b):\n    return a / b\n\n## Test Input\ndivide(1, 0)\n\n## Expected Output\ninf\n\n## VOTA Trace\n[ERROR] ZeroDivisionError: division by zero\n\n## Verdict\nfail\n\\end{verbatim}\n\\end{small}\n\n\\paragraph{Pass example.}\nNo exception is raised, and the assertion point confirms that the actual output matches the expected value.\n\n\\begin{small}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n## Function\ndef add(a, b):\n    return a + b\n\n## Test Input\nadd(2, 3)\n\n## Expected Output\n5\n\n## VOTA Trace\n[LOOP] No loops detected.\n[ASSERT] actual=5, expected=5\n\n## Verdict\npass\n\\end{verbatim}\n\\end{small}\n\nThese examples illustrate the core asymmetry exploited by the \\texttt{exception\\_only} condition: fail cases produce an explicit \\texttt{[ERROR]} event, while pass cases are characterized by its absence.\nThis binary signal---presence vs.\\ absence of an exception---is sufficient for near-perfect verdict prediction.\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{Full Experimental Results}\n\\label{sec:full_results}\n\nTables~\\ref{tab:full_qwen} and \\ref{tab:full_deepseek} report the complete experimental results across all conditions, models, and seeds.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Full results for Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B across all conditions and seeds. Conditions with a single seed report no variance. \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieves 99.83\\% across all three seeds with zero variance.}\n\\label{tab:full_qwen}\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{S42} & \\textbf{S123} & \\textbf{S456} & \\textbf{Mean $\\pm$ Std} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 99.83 & 99.83 & 99.83 & 99.83 $\\pm$ 0.00 \\\\\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}        & 99.92 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\nVOTA                     & 99.41 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\texttt{VOTA\\_no\\_ae}    & 99.41 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\texttt{label\\_only}     & 86.22 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\texttt{loop\\_only}      & 85.63 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\texttt{full\\_trace}     & 81.76 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\texttt{no\\_trace}       & 75.13 & ---   & ---   & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}%\n}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Full results for DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B across all conditions and seeds. \\texttt{exception\\_only} shows bimodal seed behavior: seeds 42 and 456 achieve 99.33\\%, while seed 123 yields 91.09\\% (LoRA initialization outlier, fail recall = 70.5\\%).}\n\\label{tab:full_deepseek}\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{Accuracy} & \\textbf{Notes} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 42  & 99.33 & epoch 1: 99.83 \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 456 & 99.33 & CI [98.82, 99.75] \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & 123 & 91.09 & fail recall = 70.5\\%; CI [89.40, 92.60] \\\\\n\\midrule\nVOTA                     & 42  & 84.45 & \\\\\n\\texttt{no\\_trace}       & 42  & 73.36 & \\\\\n\\texttt{full\\_trace}     & 42  & 79.16 & CI [76.80, 81.30] \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}%\n}\n\\end{table}\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{Irreducible Sample Analysis}\n\\label{sec:irreducible}\n\nThe \\texttt{exception\\_only} condition with Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B misclassifies exactly 2 out of 1,190 test samples across all three seeds (42, 123, 456), with 100\\% overlap across seeds.\nBoth samples (IDs 901 and 905) share the following characteristics:\n\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\textbf{Function:} \\texttt{check\\_dict\\_case} (HumanEval/95)---a function that checks whether all dictionary keys are uniformly upper-case or lower-case strings.\n\\item \\textbf{Ground-truth label:} \\texttt{pass}.\n\\item \\textbf{Predicted label:} \\texttt{fail} (false negative).\n\\item \\textbf{Cross-seed consistency:} Both samples are misclassified identically by all three seeds, indicating a systematic rather than stochastic error.\n\\end{itemize}\n\nManual inspection reveals the root cause: \\texttt{check\\_dict\\_case} uses \\texttt{all()} over a generator expression, and Python's \\texttt{all()} short-circuits on the first \\texttt{True} value, raising \\texttt{GeneratorExit} to terminate the generator.\nBecause \\texttt{GeneratorExit} is a \\texttt{BaseException} subclass, the tracer records it as seven \\texttt{[ERROR] GeneratorExit} lines---even though the function executes correctly and the test passes.\nThe model observes these spurious error markers and reasonably but incorrectly predicts \\texttt{fail}.\nThis is not a model failure but a representation ceiling: the \\texttt{exception\\_only} format cannot distinguish genuine exceptions from benign \\texttt{BaseException} subclasses triggered by Python's iterator protocol.\n\nThese 2 irreducible errors establish a practical accuracy ceiling of $1 - 2/1190 = 99.83\\%$ for the \\texttt{exception\\_only} condition on this dataset, suggesting that further improvements would require either supplementary trace signals or modifications to the input representation.\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{Trace Length Ablation}\n\\label{sec:trace_length_full}\n\nTo test whether the \\texttt{full\\_trace} performance gap is caused by input truncation at training time, we vary the maximum trace token budget on Qwen-2.5-7B (seed=42) while keeping all other hyperparameters fixed.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Max Trace Tokens} & \\textbf{Acc (\\%)} & \\textbf{95\\% CI} \\\\\n\\midrule\n256  & 78.82 & [76.4, 81.0] \\\\\n512  & 80.42 & [78.2, 82.6] \\\\\n1024 & 77.23 & [74.9, 79.6] \\\\\nno\\_limit ($\\infty$) & 81.76 & [79.7, 83.9] \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Trace length ablation on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). All four conditions fall within a 5pp range (77.2\\%--81.8\\%) with broadly overlapping confidence intervals, indicating no significant effect of trace length on full-trace accuracy.}\n\\label{tab:trace_length}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig4_trace_length}\n\\caption{Full-trace accuracy as a function of maximum trace token budget (Qwen-2.5-7B, $N{=}1{,}190$). Dashed lines show \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%), \\texttt{no\\_trace} (75.13\\%), and majority-class (71.51\\%) baselines. Accuracy remains in a narrow band regardless of trace length, confirming that trace content quality---not quantity---is the bottleneck.}\n\\label{fig:trace_length}\n\\end{figure}\n\nAs shown in Table~\\ref{tab:trace_length} and Figure~\\ref{fig:trace_length}, all four trace length conditions fall within a 5pp range (77.2\\%--81.8\\%) with broadly overlapping confidence intervals.\nThe trend is non-monotonic (1024 tokens yields the lowest accuracy at 77.23\\%), and no condition approaches the VOTA (99.41\\%) or \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%) baselines.\nThis suggests that input truncation alone does not explain the full-trace performance gap: even with unlimited trace tokens, the model cannot extract the diagnostic signal that VOTA's targeted compression provides.\nThe result reinforces the information density interpretation (\\S\\ref{sec:density}): the gap between full traces and VOTA is driven by the \\emph{content} of the trace (irrelevant lines diluting the diagnostic signal), not its \\emph{length}.\nIn contrast, VOTA's compressed representation concentrates the diagnostic signal into fewer tokens, making it sensitive to truncation when those tokens are removed (\\S\\ref{sec:cross_model}).\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{Calibration Analysis}\n\\label{sec:calibration_full}\n\nBeyond accuracy, we evaluate whether trace conditions affect prediction calibration, motivated by recent findings that code LLM confidence is often poorly calibrated \\citep{spiess2025calibration}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{ECE $\\downarrow$} & \\textbf{Brier $\\downarrow$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nae\\_only & \\textbf{0.0004} & \\textbf{0.0008} \\\\\nexception\\_only & 0.0027 & 0.0013 \\\\\nVOTA & 0.0054 & 0.005 \\\\\nfull\\_trace & 0.096 & 0.144 \\\\\nno\\_trace & 0.171 & 0.197 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Calibration metrics on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). ECE = expected calibration error; Brier = Brier score. Lower is better for both.}\n\\label{tab:calibration}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:calibration} shows that calibration quality mirrors the Tier~1/2 hierarchy.\nTier~1 conditions (\\texttt{ae\\_only}: ECE=0.0004; \\texttt{exception\\_only}: ECE=0.0027) produce near-perfectly calibrated predictions, while \\texttt{full\\_trace} (ECE=0.096) and \\texttt{no\\_trace} (ECE=0.171) exhibit substantial miscalibration.\nVOTA (ECE=0.0054) is well-calibrated but slightly worse than either Tier~1 component alone, reinforcing the pattern that combining trace signals dilutes rather than improves the prediction signal.\n\nThe Brier scores tell a consistent story: \\texttt{ae\\_only} (0.0008) and \\texttt{exception\\_only} (0.0013) achieve near-zero scores, while \\texttt{no\\_trace} (0.197) indicates substantial miscalibration.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig2_calibration}\n\\caption{Reliability diagrams for four trace conditions on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). The Tier~1 condition (\\texttt{exception\\_only}) clusters near the diagonal (well-calibrated), while \\texttt{full\\_trace} and \\texttt{no\\_trace} exhibit systematic overconfidence.}\n\\label{fig:calibration}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig3_cross_model_gap}\n\\caption{Cross-model accuracy gap (Qwen $-$ DeepSeek) across trace conditions. Exception-based signals transfer across models (0.50pp gap), while VOTA is model-dependent (14.96pp gap).}\n\\label{fig:cross_model_gap}\n\\end{figure}\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{Cross-Dataset Generalization: DebugBench}\n\\label{sec:debugbench_full}\n\nTo test whether exception-based verdict prediction generalizes beyond LLM-generated solutions, we evaluate on DebugBench \\citep{tian2024debugbench}---a dataset of human-written programs with synthetically injected bugs (single-point mutations such as operator replacement, variable misuse, and missing statements).\nAfter filtering for executable Python samples with valid traces, we retain 426 samples with a near-balanced class distribution (majority baseline: 51.4\\%).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Acc (\\%)} & \\textbf{95\\% CI} \\\\\n\\midrule\nexception\\_only & \\textbf{100.00} & [99.14, 100.00] \\\\\nVOTA & 99.77 & [99.30, 100.00] \\\\\nno\\_trace & 96.71 & [94.80, 98.40] \\\\\n\\midrule\nMajority baseline & 51.41 & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Generalization to DebugBench ($N{=}426$, Qwen-2.5-7B, seed=42). Wilson 95\\% CIs. The component ranking (exception\\_only $\\geq$ VOTA $>$ no\\_trace) is preserved, and exception\\_only achieves perfect accuracy.}\n\\label{tab:debugbench}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:debugbench} shows that the component ranking from the main experiments is preserved: \\texttt{exception\\_only} (100.00\\%) $\\geq$ VOTA (99.77\\%) $>$ \\texttt{no\\_trace} (96.71\\%).\nThe \\texttt{exception\\_only} condition achieves perfect accuracy, confirming that the exception-based representation generalizes to a different fault distribution without degradation.\n\nThe absolute margins are much narrower than on the main dataset: the \\texttt{no\\_trace} baseline already achieves 96.71\\%---compared to 75.13\\% on HumanEval+MBPP---leaving only 3.29pp of headroom for trace-based improvement.\nThis ceiling effect reflects a fundamental difference in fault characteristics: DebugBench bugs are synthetically injected single-point mutations, which produce code that is structurally dissimilar from the original correct solution; a model can often identify the fault from source code alone.\nIn contrast, the HumanEval+MBPP solutions are LLM-generated, producing natural errors (logic mistakes, edge-case failures) where the faulty and correct solutions may be structurally similar, making code-only verdict prediction substantially harder.\n\nThese results support exception-based verdict prediction as a generalizable approach---the method does not degrade on an out-of-distribution dataset, and the exception dominance ranking (exception\\_only $\\geq$ VOTA $>$ no\\_trace) is consistent across both datasets---but the narrow DebugBench margins are insufficient to independently validate trace importance.\nThe 24.70pp trace lift on the main dataset (no\\_trace 75.13\\% $\\to$ exception\\_only 99.83\\%) remains the primary evidence for the value of exception-based trace signals.\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{Cross-Model Robustness Analysis}\n\\label{sec:robustness_analysis}\n\nThe cross-model gap pattern (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}) suggests a design principle: \\emph{trace representations should match the model capability floor, not the ceiling.}\nException events are consistently the most effective component on both models, and their structural simplicity---a fixed-format error message---ensures that the relative advantage over richer representations holds across all seeds.\nTwo of three DeepSeek seeds match Qwen-level accuracy (99.33\\%); the outlier (seed 123, 91.09\\%) is attributable to LoRA initialization landing in a suboptimal basin (fail recall = 70.5\\%), not to a failure of the exception signal itself---even this outlier outperforms VOTA (84.45\\%) by 6.64pp.\nThe VOTA cross-model gap is confounded by two factors: (1)~tokenizer efficiency---the DeepSeek tokenizer averages 2{,}626 tokens per VOTA sample versus Qwen's 1{,}929, causing 91.7\\% of training samples to exceed the 2{,}048-token context window; content-aware truncation preferentially removes the terminal structured prompt, preserving the verdict label but truncating trace content---and (2)~base model capability differences, consistent with findings that SFT generalization depends on pretrain quality \\citep{yang2024generalization}.\nThe two factors cannot be disentangled in the current experimental design.\nOur \\texttt{exception\\_only} design sidesteps the truncation confound entirely: inputs average ${\\sim}$200 tokens, well below the 2{,}048-token limit, so no training samples are truncated on either model.\nThe resulting 0.50pp cross-model gap (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}) is therefore a clean measure of model capability differences, making the ``capability floor'' principle directly testable.\nThe practical implication is that trace-based systems should default to the simplest sufficient signal; for smaller models, training stability (multi-seed selection, warmup schedules) is a secondary but addressable concern.\n\n%% ============================================================\n\\section{Training Dynamics and Loss Decoupling}\n\\label{sec:training_dynamics}\n\nThe DeepSeek \\texttt{exception\\_only} results reveal two instructive training dynamics.\nFirst, epoch-level analysis shows mild degradation in 2/3 seeds: seed 42 peaks at 99.83\\% after epoch~1 but settles at 99.33\\% by epoch~3 ($-$0.50pp), seed 123 degrades from 91.43\\% to 91.09\\% ($-$0.34pp), while seed 456 shows no epoch degradation (99.33\\% at both epoch~1 and epoch~3).\nEarly stopping may yield marginal gains for some seeds, but the epoch effect is small (${\\leq}$0.5pp) compared to the seed effect (8.24pp between seeds 42 and 123).\n\nSecond, seeds 123 and 456 converge to nearly identical final training losses (0.122 and 0.123 respectively), yet their accuracies differ by 8.24pp (91.09\\% vs.\\ 99.33\\%).\nThe critical difference lies in fail recall: seeds 42 and 456 achieve 100\\%, while seed 123 achieves only 70.5\\%---the LoRA initialization in seed 123 fails to learn the exception$\\to$fail mapping for a substantial fraction of failing samples, even though the per-token loss appears converged.\nThis demonstrates that token-level training loss can mask fundamental differences in sample-level classification ability, suggesting a parallel to the eval-loss/accuracy decoupling observed in DeepSeek VOTA (where eval loss rose 65\\% from epoch 1 to 3 while accuracy improved by 6.3pp) at the training phase.\nThe practical recommendation is twofold: (1)~use task-specific metrics (accuracy, fail recall) for checkpoint and seed selection rather than training or evaluation loss, and (2)~for deployment on smaller models, train multiple seeds and select the best, as a single run may not reliably converge to the correct classification boundary.\n\nThe contrast with VOTA is instructive: DeepSeek VOTA accuracy monotonically increases from 78.15\\% to 84.45\\% across epochs, but is consistent with a performance limit associated with the high trace truncation rate (\\S\\ref{sec:cross_model})---the model progressively extracts signal from samples with intact trace content but cannot fully compensate for the information loss in the truncated majority.\nThe 84.45\\% accuracy reflects a combination of trace information loss and model capability constraints.\n",

```text
1	# Paper Framing — trace-verdict-study
2	
3	> v3.0 — [UPDATED 2026-05-15] D030 CLOSED: DeepSeek VOTA 84.45% confirmed correct (confirmatory re-eval). **Critical narrative fix**: to_chat_text() preserves labels → VOTA 训练时 verdict label 未被截断 → 论文中 "label truncation/corruption" 措辞对 VOTA 不成立。正确叙事: trace INPUT content 被截断 (91.7% samples), 84.45% 反映 trace information loss。新增 D11: VOTA cross-model gap confounded by training-time truncation。数据集来源：HumanEval (164) + MBPP-sanitized (427) = 591 problems × 10 LLM-generated solutions = 5910 samples。
4	
5	## Target Venue
6	- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)
7	- Fallback: EMNLP 2026 Findings
8	- Deadline: ARR May 25, 2026
9	
10	## Core Insight
11	
12	Verdict prediction (pass/fail) is fundamentally a **fault detection** task. The most effective signal is the **exception event** — a compact, structured diagnostic moment that consistently outperforms all other trace representations across models (Qwen 99.83% ± 0.00 across 3 seeds; DeepSeek 91-99% depending on seed, but always > VOTA 84.45% and no_trace 73.36%). In contrast, richer trace representations that require deeper execution reasoning show model-dependent effectiveness: VOTA gap = 14.96pp, no_trace gap = 1.77pp. Information density — not training signal distribution — is the primary bottleneck: even oracle loss masking (label-only) cannot close the 13pp gap between full traces and targeted extraction. The design principle: **extract the highest-specificity diagnostic signal; discard everything else.** Cross-model absolute performance depends on LoRA initialization stability, but exception_only's relative dominance over other components is robust.
13	
14	VOTA's primary contribution is **fault indicator isolation** — automatically extracting exception events from noisy full traces — rather than generic trace compression. The "compression helps" narrative was a stepping stone; the real mechanism is diagnostic signal extraction.
15	
16	## Core Claims (3)
17	
18	### Claim 1: Exception events are the most effective trace component for verdict prediction
19	
20	Exception summaries consistently achieve the highest verdict prediction accuracy across both models tested, outperforming all other trace representations in every seed. On Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B, performance is perfectly robust: 99.83% ± 0.00 (3 seeds). On DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B, absolute accuracy is seed-dependent (seed42=99.83% epoch1, seed123=91.43% epoch1; Δ=8.40pp), consistent with LoRA initialization sensitivity — but exception_only still dominates VOTA (84.45%) and no_trace (73.36%) in all seeds. This contrasts with VOTA (cross-model gap = 14.96pp) and no_trace (gap = 1.77pp). Exception events are structurally simple (`[ERROR] type: ... message: ...`) and do not require deep trace reasoning.
21	
22	**Supporting evidence:**
23	- `vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`: Qwen 99.83%, ECE=0.0027
24	- `deepseek_exception_only_seed42`: DeepSeek 99.83% (epoch1) / 99.33% (epoch3)
25	- `deepseek_exception_only_seed456`: DeepSeek 99.33% (epoch3), CI [98.82, 99.75] — 与 seed42 完全一致
26	- `deepseek_exception_only_seed123`: DeepSeek 91.43% (epoch1) / 91.09% (epoch3) — LoRA init sensitivity outlier
27	- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: DeepSeek VOTA 84.45% (gap = 14.96pp vs Qwen)
28	- `deepseek_no_trace_seed42`: DeepSeek no_trace 73.36% (gap = 1.77pp vs Qwen)
29	- `vota_no_ae_seed42`: 99.41% — actual/expected removal 不降，非 trivial string comparison
30	
31	**DeepSeek seed analysis** ✅ (FINALIZED, D029):
32	
33	| Seed | Epoch 3 (final) | fail_recall | pass_recall | train_loss | Errors | CI |
34	|------|-----------------|-------------|-------------|------------|--------|-----|
35	| 42   | 99.33%          | 1.0         | 0.9906      | —          | 8/1190 | — |
36	| 456  | 99.33%          | 1.0         | 0.9906      | 0.1225     | 8/1190 | [98.82, 99.75] |
37	| 123  | 91.09%          | 0.705       | 0.993       | 0.122      | —      | [89.4, 92.6] |
38	
39	seed42 与 seed456 结果**完全一致**（均 8/1190 错误，99.33%），seed123 为离群值。seed123 的 fail_recall 仅 0.705 — LoRA 初始化落入差的局部最优，未充分学到 exception→fail 映射。**关键发现**: seed456 和 seed123 的 train_loss 几乎相同（0.1225 vs 0.122），但 accuracy 差 8.2pp — token-level loss 掩盖了 sample-level 分类能力的巨大差异（详见 D10）。
40	
41	**Irreducible error analysis** ✅: 3 seeds 错误的 2 个样本 (id=901, 905) 均来自 HumanEval/95 (`check_dict_case`)，根因为 Python `all()` 对 generator expression 短路触发 `GeneratorExit`（`BaseException` 子类，非真正错误）。tracer 将其标记为 `[ERROR]`，exception_only 模型看到 7 行 `[ERROR] GeneratorExit`，合理但错误地预测 fail。整个 1190 条测试集仅这 2 条含 `GeneratorExit`，模型无训练信号学习其良性本质。**这确认 99.83% 是 exception_only 表征的理论上限，剩余 0.17% 是 representation-inherent irreducible error，而非模型不足。**
42	
43	**Seed robustness** ✅: Qwen exception_only 3-seed 完全一致，构成本文最强 robustness 证据：
44	
45	| Seed | Accuracy | 95% CI | Errors |
46	|------|----------|--------|--------|
47	| 42 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |
48	| 123 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |
49	| 456 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |
50	
51	3 seeds 错误样本 100% 重叠（id=901, 905, 均为 `check_dict_case` 函数, label=pass → pred=fail）。**Irreducible samples**: 模型已达数据集理论上限，variance = 0。
52	
53	**DeepSeek cross-model** ✅ (D029): 3-seed 完整数据 — seed42/456=99.33%, seed123=91.09%。2/3 seeds 鲁棒，seed123 为 LoRA 初始化离群值（fail_recall 仅 0.705）。Qwen 3-seed 完美稳定 (99.83% ± 0.00) vs DeepSeek 高方差（σ≈4.8pp），暗示 exception signal 的 learnability 与模型 LoRA 训练稳定性相关。但即使最差 seed (91.09%) 仍优于 VOTA (84.45%) 和 no_trace (73.36%)，relative dominance robust。
54	
55	### Claim 2: Information density, not gradient dilution, drives trace compression benefit
56	
57	Full traces help verdict prediction (81.76% [`full_trace_seed42_v5`], +6.6pp over baseline), but VOTA achieves 99.41% [`vota_seed42_v5`] (+24.3pp) — a +17.6pp advantage. Label-only loss ablation (plan_002) quantifies the causal decomposition: **gradient dilution contributes ~26% of the gap** (4.5pp; label-only full_trace = 86.22% [`full_trace_label_only_seed42`]), **information overload contributes ~74%** (13.0pp; only trace compression resolves this). Even with oracle loss masking that eliminates gradient dilution, full traces still underperform VOTA by 13pp.
58	
59	Cross-model evidence reinforces this: VOTA gap (14.96pp) partly tracks base model execution reasoning capability (Qwen CRUXEval-I 56.5% vs DeepSeek 39.0%, Δ=17.5pp), though **confounded by DeepSeek training-time trace truncation** (91.7% of VOTA samples truncated to ~2048 tokens; see D11). The two factors cannot be disentangled in the current design. Exception signal partially bypasses both bottlenecks (token-short, no truncation; consistently best component on both models), though DeepSeek shows seed-dependent absolute performance (91-99%).
60	
61	**Supporting evidence:**
62	- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (gradient dilution fix, +4.5pp over standard full_trace)
63	- `full_trace_seed42_v5`: 81.76%, ECE=0.0958 (standard full_trace)
64	- `vota_seed42_v5`: 99.41%, ECE=0.0054
65	- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (VOTA model-dependent)
66	- CRUXEval literature data (Wang et al.)
67	
68	### Claim 3: Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy for verdict prediction
69	
70	Component ablation reveals verdict-relevant trace information has a strict two-tier hierarchy:
71	- **Tier 1 — diagnostic moment signals**: exception summaries (99.83% [`vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`]) and assertion-point information (99.92% [`vota_ae_only_seed42`]) are individually near-perfect and sufficient for verdict prediction on Qwen. These signals are structurally simple and consistently the most effective component on both models, though DeepSeek absolute performance is seed-dependent.
72	- **Tier 2 — behavioral patterns**: loop compression (85.63% [`vota_loop_only_seed42`]) carries substantial signal but is model-dependent and fully subsumed by Tier 1.
73	
74	Combining tiers introduces noise rather than helping: exception_only (99.83%) > VOTA full (99.41%), p=0.01. Loop_only ≈ full_trace_label_only (85.63% vs 86.22%) confirms VOTA's loop compression precisely extracts the useful behavioral signal from full traces.
75	
76	**Supporting evidence:**
77	- `vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`: 99.83%, ECE=0.0027 (Tier 1 exception)
78	- `vota_ae_only_seed42`: 99.92%, ECE=0.0004 (Tier 1 assertion-point, 与 exception 统计等价 p=0.80)
79	- `vota_loop_only_seed42`: 85.63%, ECE=0.0831 (Tier 2 behavioral)
80	- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (≈ loop_only, confirming extraction equivalence)
81	- `deepseek_exception_only_seed42`: 99.83% epoch1 / 99.33% epoch3 (Tier 1 cross-model validation)
82	- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (Tier 1+2 cross-model: Tier 2 introduces model-dependent degradation)
83	
84	**Calibration**: 完整 calibration 数据确认 Tier 1 信号不仅 accuracy 近完美，置信度校准也近完美：
85	
86	| Condition | Accuracy | ECE | Brier | 解读 |
87	|-----------|----------|-----|-------|------|
88	| ae_only | 99.92% | 0.0004 | 0.0008 | 近乎完美校准 |
89	| exception_only | 99.83% | 0.0027 | 0.0013 | 近乎完美校准 |
90	| VOTA | 99.41% | 0.0054 | 0.005 | 优秀，但 Tier 2 引入轻微 noise |
91	| loop_only | 85.63% | 0.0831 | — | Tier 2 单独校准中等 |
92	| label_only | 86.22% | 0.1107 | — | gradient dilution fix 仍 miscalibrated |
93	| full_trace | 81.76% | 0.0958 | — | 中度 miscalibrated |
94	| no_trace | 75.13% | 0.1715 | 0.1972 | 严重 miscalibrated |
95	
96	no_trace ECE 是 exception_only 的 **~63x**（0.1715/0.0027），Brier 是 **~150x**（0.1972/0.0013）。Trace signal（尤其 Tier 1）不仅提升 accuracy，更从根本上改善模型 calibration。
97	
98	**Design principle**: verdict-optimized traces should extract highest-specificity diagnostic signals; lower-specificity signals are redundant and may introduce noise.
99	
100	## Cross-Model Evidence Summary
101	
102	| Condition | Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B | DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B | Cross-model gap | Interpretation |
103	|-----------|-------------------|---------------------|-----------------|----------------|
104	| exception_only | 99.83% ± 0.00 (3 seeds) | seed42/456: 99.33%; seed123: 91.09% | 0.50-8.74pp (seed-dependent) | **Best component on both models**; 2/3 DeepSeek seeds match Qwen-level; 1 outlier still > VOTA |
105	| VOTA | 99.41% | 84.45% | -14.96pp | Model-dependent — requires execution reasoning; **also confounded by DeepSeek training-time trace truncation** (91.7% of VOTA samples truncated from mean 2626 to ~2048 tokens; labels preserved via to_chat_text(), see D11) |
106	| no_trace | 75.13% | 73.36% | -1.77pp | Baseline gap tracks general capability |
107	| full_trace | 81.76% | collapsed (v3 pending) | N/A | DeepSeek v1 label truncation bug |
108	
109	**Key insight**: Exception_only is consistently the best component on both models (even worst DeepSeek seed 91.09% > VOTA 84.45%). Cross-model gap 与 signal complexity 正相关, but exception signal's absolute effectiveness also depends on LoRA training stability. Qwen: perfectly robust (3 seeds identical). DeepSeek: 2/3 seeds at 99.33% (matching Qwen-level), 1 outlier at 91.09% (LoRA initialization). Median performance robust; seed sensitivity suggests smaller/weaker models may need training stabilization (multi-seed selection, warmup tuning) for optimal exception signal utilization.
110	
111	## VOTA Repositioning
112	
113	**旧叙事**: VOTA 是 trace compression 方法，价值在于"压缩使 trace 更有效"。
114	
115	**新叙事**: VOTA 是 **fault indicator isolation** 方法，核心价值在于自动从 noisy execution traces 中提取 exception events 等 diagnostic signals。
116	
117	**证据链**:
118	1. exception_only (99.83%) ≥ VOTA full (99.41%) — 压缩后的其他组件（loop, ae）不仅无益，反而引入 noise
119	2. VOTA 跨模型 gap = 14.96pp — 因为 VOTA 包含 model-dependent 的 Tier 2 组件（loop compression 需要 execution reasoning）
120	3. exception_only 在两个模型上均为最优组件 — isolate 出 Tier 1 后，相对优势 robust（虽然 DeepSeek 绝对值 seed-dependent）
121	4. vota_no_ae (99.41%) = VOTA (99.41%) — actual/expected 移除无影响，exception 是真正的 active ingredient
122	
123	**论文中的表述**: "VOTA's trace compression pipeline incidentally isolates exception events — the minimal sufficient diagnostic signal. Its value lies not in making traces shorter, but in separating high-specificity fault indicators from low-specificity behavioral patterns."
124	
125	**对 Method section 的影响**: VOTA 仍然完整描述（loop compression + exception + actual/expected），但 Results/Discussion 明确指出 exception isolation 是核心机制，其他组件是 model-capability-dependent bonus。
126	
127	## Story Spine
128	
129	- **Problem**: Existing trace SFT studies (DCSH, TracePile) evaluate only generative tasks and find limited, model-dependent benefit. No prior work has tested trace SFT on verdict tasks (binary pass/fail), nor identified what trace components drive classification performance.
130	
131	- **Existing approaches & gaps**: DCSH (EMNLP Findings 2025) tested 5 trace formats on generation tasks, finding that "no single trace representation consistently outperforms others." TracePile (NeurIPS 2025) scaled to 2.6M samples but only evaluated generation. TRACED (ICSE 2024) showed traces help encoder-based classification. All existing trace formats are designed for execution simulation, not fault detection.
132	
133	- **Our framing**: We reframe verdict prediction as **fault detection** and ask: what is the minimal sufficient diagnostic signal? Through systematic component ablation across two architecturally distinct models, we discover that exception events — compact, structured error summaries — are consistently the most effective trace component, achieving near-perfect accuracy on Qwen (99.83% ± 0.00, 3 seeds) and outperforming all alternatives on DeepSeek (91-99% vs VOTA 84.45%). Richer trace representations work only when the base model has sufficient execution reasoning capability, making them unreliable for general deployment.
134	
135	- **Headline result**: "Exception events consistently achieve the highest verdict accuracy across models (Qwen 99.83%, DeepSeek 91-99%), outperforming VOTA traces (84.45% on DeepSeek, -14.96pp gap) — demonstrating that fault indicator isolation, not trace reasoning, is the robust mechanism for verdict prediction. DeepSeek seed sensitivity reveals LoRA training stability as a secondary factor for smaller models."
136	
137	## Story Arc
138	
139	1. **Setup**: Trace SFT for code tasks — promising but inconsistent in prior work (DCSH, TracePile). Unknown whether traces help verdict tasks and what trace design matters.
140	
141	2. **First result — traces help, compression helps more** (single model): no_trace 75.13% → full_trace 81.76% → VOTA 99.41%. Label-only loss ablation decomposes the gap: 26% gradient dilution, 74% information overload. Compression improves accuracy through higher information density, not just cost savings.
142	
143	3. **Twist — what component actually drives VOTA?** Component ablation reveals exception_only (99.83%) ≥ full VOTA (99.41%). The "trace reasoning" narrative is wrong — verdict prediction is fault detection. Tier 1 (exceptions + assertion-point) subsumes Tier 2 (loops). Actual/expected removal doesn't hurt (99.41%).
144	
145	4. **Cross-model stress test — consistent dominance, not universal perfection**: Exception_only is the best component on both models (even DeepSeek worst seed 91.09% > VOTA 84.45%), but absolute performance is seed-dependent on DeepSeek (91-99% vs Qwen's perfectly stable 99.83%). VOTA degrades by 14.96pp. The structurally simplest component is the most robust, but LoRA initialization sensitivity introduces variance on weaker models. Qwen seed robustness (3-seed variance = 0) + calibration (ECE=0.0027) 确认 exception signal 在强模型上的 reliability。DeepSeek seed sensitivity 揭示 training stability 作为 secondary bottleneck。
146	
147	5. **Design principle**: For verdict tasks, identify the highest-specificity diagnostic signal (exception events) and discard everything else. VOTA's value is fault indicator isolation — automatically extracting exceptions from noisy traces — not generic compression. Simpler signals are more robust; complexity tolerance depends on model capability.
148	
149	## Dataset Characteristics
150	
151	### Data duplication analysis
152	
153	| Condition | Total | Unique | Duplication rate | Cause |
154	|-----------|-------|--------|------------------|-------|
155	| exception_only_train | 4720 | 2858 | 39.45% | 同题多个 passing 解映射为相同 (input, output) — 无异常 → 同一 trace |
156	| exception_only_test | 1190 | 607 | 48.99% | 同上 |
157	| raw_traces | 5910 | 5803 | 1.81% | 完整 trace 含行号/变量值/内存地址，几乎不重复 |
158	
159	**Train-test input overlap: 0（无数据泄漏）**
160	
161	**解读**: exception_only 高重复率是 representation 的自然属性，非数据问题。多个正确解对同一测试用例执行时若均无异常，exception_only 表征退化为相同的空/无异常标记。这恰好说明 exception signal 的 compactness — 它仅编码 fault presence，不编码 solution identity。raw_traces 重复率极低 (1.81%) 因为完整 trace 包含 solution-specific 信息（变量值、内存地址）。尽管高重复率意味着有效训练样本少于名义数量，模型仍达 99.83% — 进一步确认 exception signal 的 learnability。
162	
163	## DeepSeek full_trace 处理
164	
165	**现状**: v1 坍塌（28.5%, 100% 预测 fail, 根因: 训练端 label 截断）。v2 重训启动但被 kill（Loop #6 ops worker 腾 GPU 给 vota_seed456）。v3 RUNNING（label-preserving truncation fix）。
166	
167	**处理方案**: v3 正在 cuda:0 上训练，预计 5/16 完成。
168	
169	**VOTA vs full_trace 截断机制区分** (D030 token analysis 确认):
170	- **full_trace v1**: SFTTrainer right-truncation 切掉了 verdict **label** → 100% predict fail → 坍塌。根因: DeepSeek tokenizer 对 full_trace 产生 >2048 tokens, label 在截断窗口外。
171	- **VOTA**: `to_chat_text()` 保留 verdict label（label 完整），但 91.7% 的样本 **trace content（input）被截断**（mean 2626 → ~2048 tokens）。84.45% 是真实结果，反映 trace 信息损失对模型学习的影响，**不是** label corruption。
172	- **exception_only**: token 长度远低于 2048，不受截断影响，跨模型对比 clean。
173	
174	**对论文叙事的影响**: 论文中所有 "right-truncation corrupts/removes the verdict label" 措辞**对 VOTA 不成立**——label 完整保留，被截断的是 trace input。正确表述: "trace content truncation causes information loss"。VOTA cross-model gap (14.96pp) 有两个贡献因素: (1) model capability, (2) training-time trace truncation on DeepSeek (see D11)。
175	
176	## External Benchmark (DebugBench) Positioning
177	
178	**DebugBench 仅作泛化性验证，不作 trace importance 证据。**
179	
180	| Condition | Main Dataset | DebugBench | Δ interpretation |
181	|-----------|-------------|------------|------------------|
182	| no_trace | 75.13% | 96.71% | DebugBench 天花板高，baseline 已强 |
183	| VOTA | 99.41% | 99.77% | +3.06pp lift — 边际 |
184	| exception_only | 99.83% | 100.00% | +3.29pp lift — 边际 |
185	
186	**主数据集 trace lift = 24.70pp**（no_trace → exception_only），DebugBench trace lift 不到其 1/7。
187	
188	**论文定位规则**：
189	- DebugBench → 泛化性证据（exception_only 100% = 跨数据集鲁棒性）
190	- Trace importance 论证 → 仅依赖主数据集（24.7pp lift）
191	- 禁止用 DebugBench 的 3.29pp lift 论证 "trace 的核心价值"——审稿人会攻击天花板效应
192	
193	## Venue Fit Risks
194	
195	| Risk | Assessment | Mitigation |
196	|------|-----------|------------|
197	| 单任务 | 仍然只在 test verdict prediction 上验证 | 跨模型验证部分弥补; Discussion 中讨论 generalization |
198	| 单数据集 | HumanEval + MBPP-sanitized 是唯一数据源 (591 problems × 10 solutions) | 定位为 empirical study, 深度优于广度; 两个标准 benchmark 组合覆盖多样编程题型; exception_only 有效训练样本仅 2858 unique 即达 99.83%, 暗示 signal 的 simplicity 降低对数据量的依赖 |
199	| "只是 feature engineering" | exception extraction 不复杂 | simplicity 本身是 contribution — 最简信号最 robust |
200	| Negative result 味道 | VOTA 跨模型不 robust | Reframe: 识别 robust vs non-robust 组件边界是 positive |
```

```json
      "content": "# Paper Framing — trace-verdict-study\n\n> v3.0 — [UPDATED 2026-05-15] D030 CLOSED: DeepSeek VOTA 84.45% confirmed correct (confirmatory re-eval). **Critical narrative fix**: to_chat_text() preserves labels → VOTA 训练时 verdict label 未被截断 → 论文中 \"label truncation/corruption\" 措辞对 VOTA 不成立。正确叙事: trace INPUT content 被截断 (91.7% samples), 84.45% 反映 trace information loss。新增 D11: VOTA cross-model gap confounded by training-time truncation。数据集来源：HumanEval (164) + MBPP-sanitized (427) = 591 problems × 10 LLM-generated solutions = 5910 samples。\n\n## Target Venue\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)\n- Fallback: EMNLP 2026 Findings\n- Deadline: ARR May 25, 2026\n\n## Core Insight\n\nVerdict prediction (pass/fail) is fundamentally a **fault detection** task. The most effective signal is the **exception event** — a compact, structured diagnostic moment that consistently outperforms all other trace representations across models (Qwen 99.83% ± 0.00 across 3 seeds; DeepSeek 91-99% depending on seed, but always > VOTA 84.45% and no_trace 73.36%). In contrast, richer trace representations that require deeper execution reasoning show model-dependent effectiveness: VOTA gap = 14.96pp, no_trace gap = 1.77pp. Information density — not training signal distribution — is the primary bottleneck: even oracle loss masking (label-only) cannot close the 13pp gap between full traces and targeted extraction. The design principle: **extract the highest-specificity diagnostic signal; discard everything else.** Cross-model absolute performance depends on LoRA initialization stability, but exception_only's relative dominance over other components is robust.\n\nVOTA's primary contribution is **fault indicator isolation** — automatically extracting exception events from noisy full traces — rather than generic trace compression. The \"compression helps\" narrative was a stepping stone; the real mechanism is diagnostic signal extraction.\n\n## Core Claims (3)\n\n### Claim 1: Exception events are the most effective trace component for verdict prediction\n\nException summaries consistently achieve the highest verdict prediction accuracy across both models tested, outperforming all other trace representations in every seed. On Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B, performance is perfectly robust: 99.83% ± 0.00 (3 seeds). On DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B, absolute accuracy is seed-dependent (seed42=99.83% epoch1, seed123=91.43% epoch1; Δ=8.40pp), consistent with LoRA initialization sensitivity — but exception_only still dominates VOTA (84.45%) and no_trace (73.36%) in all seeds. This contrasts with VOTA (cross-model gap = 14.96pp) and no_trace (gap = 1.77pp). Exception events are structurally simple (`[ERROR] type: ... message: ...`) and do not require deep trace reasoning.\n\n**Supporting evidence:**\n- `vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`: Qwen 99.83%, ECE=0.0027\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed42`: DeepSeek 99.83% (epoch1) / 99.33% (epoch3)\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed456`: DeepSeek 99.33% (epoch3), CI [98.82, 99.75] — 与 seed42 完全一致\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed123`: DeepSeek 91.43% (epoch1) / 91.09% (epoch3) — LoRA init sensitivity outlier\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: DeepSeek VOTA 84.45% (gap = 14.96pp vs Qwen)\n- `deepseek_no_trace_seed42`: DeepSeek no_trace 73.36% (gap = 1.77pp vs Qwen)\n- `vota_no_ae_seed42`: 99.41% — actual/expected removal 不降，非 trivial string comparison\n\n**DeepSeek seed analysis** ✅ (FINALIZED, D029):\n\n| Seed | Epoch 3 (final) | fail_recall | pass_recall | train_loss | Errors | CI |\n|------|-----------------|-------------|-------------|------------|--------|-----|\n| 42   | 99.33%          | 1.0         | 0.9906      | —          | 8/1190 | — |\n| 456  | 99.33%          | 1.0         | 0.9906      | 0.1225     | 8/1190 | [98.82, 99.75] |\n| 123  | 91.09%          | 0.705       | 0.993       | 0.122      | —      | [89.4, 92.6] |\n\nseed42 与 seed456 结果**完全一致**（均 8/1190 错误，99.33%），seed123 为离群值。seed123 的 fail_recall 仅 0.705 — LoRA 初始化落入差的局部最优，未充分学到 exception→fail 映射。**关键发现**: seed456 和 seed123 的 train_loss 几乎相同（0.1225 vs 0.122），但 accuracy 差 8.2pp — token-level loss 掩盖了 sample-level 分类能力的巨大差异（详见 D10）。\n\n**Irreducible error analysis** ✅: 3 seeds 错误的 2 个样本 (id=901, 905) 均来自 HumanEval/95 (`check_dict_case`)，根因为 Python `all()` 对 generator expression 短路触发 `GeneratorExit`（`BaseException` 子类，非真正错误）。tracer 将其标记为 `[ERROR]`，exception_only 模型看到 7 行 `[ERROR] GeneratorExit`，合理但错误地预测 fail。整个 1190 条测试集仅这 2 条含 `GeneratorExit`，模型无训练信号学习其良性本质。**这确认 99.83% 是 exception_only 表征的理论上限，剩余 0.17% 是 representation-inherent irreducible error，而非模型不足。**\n\n**Seed robustness** ✅: Qwen exception_only 3-seed 完全一致，构成本文最强 robustness 证据：\n\n| Seed | Accuracy | 95% CI | Errors |\n|------|----------|--------|--------|\n| 42 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |\n| 123 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |\n| 456 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |\n\n3 seeds 错误样本 100% 重叠（id=901, 905, 均为 `check_dict_case` 函数, label=pass → pred=fail）。**Irreducible samples**: 模型已达数据集理论上限，variance = 0。\n\n**DeepSeek cross-model** ✅ (D029): 3-seed 完整数据 — seed42/456=99.33%, seed123=91.09%。2/3 seeds 鲁棒，seed123 为 LoRA 初始化离群值（fail_recall 仅 0.705）。Qwen 3-seed 完美稳定 (99.83% ± 0.00) vs DeepSeek 高方差（σ≈4.8pp），暗示 exception signal 的 learnability 与模型 LoRA 训练稳定性相关。但即使最差 seed (91.09%) 仍优于 VOTA (84.45%) 和 no_trace (73.36%)，relative dominance robust。\n\n### Claim 2: Information density, not gradient dilution, drives trace compression benefit\n\nFull traces help verdict prediction (81.76% [`full_trace_seed42_v5`], +6.6pp over baseline), but VOTA achieves 99.41% [`vota_seed42_v5`] (+24.3pp) — a +17.6pp advantage. Label-only loss ablation (plan_002) quantifies the causal decomposition: **gradient dilution contributes ~26% of the gap** (4.5pp; label-only full_trace = 86.22% [`full_trace_label_only_seed42`]), **information overload contributes ~74%** (13.0pp; only trace compression resolves this). Even with oracle loss masking that eliminates gradient dilution, full traces still underperform VOTA by 13pp.\n\nCross-model evidence reinforces this: VOTA gap (14.96pp) partly tracks base model execution reasoning capability (Qwen CRUXEval-I 56.5% vs DeepSeek 39.0%, Δ=17.5pp), though **confounded by DeepSeek training-time trace truncation** (91.7% of VOTA samples truncated to ~2048 tokens; see D11). The two factors cannot be disentangled in the current design. Exception signal partially bypasses both bottlenecks (token-short, no truncation; consistently best component on both models), though DeepSeek shows seed-dependent absolute performance (91-99%).\n\n**Supporting evidence:**\n- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (gradient dilution fix, +4.5pp over standard full_trace)\n- `full_trace_seed42_v5`: 81.76%, ECE=0.0958 (standard full_trace)\n- `vota_seed42_v5`: 99.41%, ECE=0.0054\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (VOTA model-dependent)\n- CRUXEval literature data (Wang et al.)\n\n### Claim 3: Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy for verdict prediction\n\nComponent ablation reveals verdict-relevant trace information has a strict two-tier hierarchy:\n- **Tier 1 — diagnostic moment signals**: exception summaries (99.83% [`vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`]) and assertion-point information (99.92% [`vota_ae_only_seed42`]) are individually near-perfect and sufficient for verdict prediction on Qwen. These signals are structurally simple and consistently the most effective component on both models, though DeepSeek absolute performance is seed-dependent.\n- **Tier 2 — behavioral patterns**: loop compression (85.63% [`vota_loop_only_seed42`]) carries substantial signal but is model-dependent and fully subsumed by Tier 1.\n\nCombining tiers introduces noise rather than helping: exception_only (99.83%) > VOTA full (99.41%), p=0.01. Loop_only ≈ full_trace_label_only (85.63% vs 86.22%) confirms VOTA's loop compression precisely extracts the useful behavioral signal from full traces.\n\n**Supporting evidence:**\n- `vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`: 99.83%, ECE=0.0027 (Tier 1 exception)\n- `vota_ae_only_seed42`: 99.92%, ECE=0.0004 (Tier 1 assertion-point, 与 exception 统计等价 p=0.80)\n- `vota_loop_only_seed42`: 85.63%, ECE=0.0831 (Tier 2 behavioral)\n- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (≈ loop_only, confirming extraction equivalence)\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed42`: 99.83% epoch1 / 99.33% epoch3 (Tier 1 cross-model validation)\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (Tier 1+2 cross-model: Tier 2 introduces model-dependent degradation)\n\n**Calibration**: 完整 calibration 数据确认 Tier 1 信号不仅 accuracy 近完美，置信度校准也近完美：\n\n| Condition | Accuracy | ECE | Brier | 解读 |\n|-----------|----------|-----|-------|------|\n| ae_only | 99.92% | 0.0004 | 0.0008 | 近乎完美校准 |\n| exception_only | 99.83% | 0.0027 | 0.0013 | 近乎完美校准 |\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 0.0054 | 0.005 | 优秀，但 Tier 2 引入轻微 noise |\n| loop_only | 85.63% | 0.0831 | — | Tier 2 单独校准中等 |\n| label_only | 86.22% | 0.1107 | — | gradient dilution fix 仍 miscalibrated |\n| full_trace | 81.76% | 0.0958 | — | 中度 miscalibrated |\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 0.1715 | 0.1972 | 严重 miscalibrated |\n\nno_trace ECE 是 exception_only 的 **~63x**（0.1715/0.0027），Brier 是 **~150x**（0.1972/0.0013）。Trace signal（尤其 Tier 1）不仅提升 accuracy，更从根本上改善模型 calibration。\n\n**Design principle**: verdict-optimized traces should extract highest-specificity diagnostic signals; lower-specificity signals are redundant and may introduce noise.\n\n## Cross-Model Evidence Summary\n\n| Condition | Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B | DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B | Cross-model gap | Interpretation |\n|-----------|-------------------|---------------------|-----------------|----------------|\n| exception_only | 99.83% ± 0.00 (3 seeds) | seed42/456: 99.33%; seed123: 91.09% | 0.50-8.74pp (seed-dependent) | **Best component on both models**; 2/3 DeepSeek seeds match Qwen-level; 1 outlier still > VOTA |\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 84.45% | -14.96pp | Model-dependent — requires execution reasoning; **also confounded by DeepSeek training-time trace truncation** (91.7% of VOTA samples truncated from mean 2626 to ~2048 tokens; labels preserved via to_chat_text(), see D11) |\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 73.36% | -1.77pp | Baseline gap tracks general capability |\n| full_trace | 81.76% | collapsed (v3 pending) | N/A | DeepSeek v1 label truncation bug |\n\n**Key insight**: Exception_only is consistently the best component on both models (even worst DeepSeek seed 91.09% > VOTA 84.45%). Cross-model gap 与 signal complexity 正相关, but exception signal's absolute effectiveness also depends on LoRA training stability. Qwen: perfectly robust (3 seeds identical). DeepSeek: 2/3 seeds at 99.33% (matching Qwen-level), 1 outlier at 91.09% (LoRA initialization). Median performance robust; seed sensitivity suggests smaller/weaker models may need training stabilization (multi-seed selection, warmup tuning) for optimal exception signal utilization.\n\n## VOTA Repositioning\n\n**旧叙事**: VOTA 是 trace compression 方法，价值在于\"压缩使 trace 更有效\"。\n\n**新叙事**: VOTA 是 **fault indicator isolation** 方法，核心价值在于自动从 noisy execution traces 中提取 exception events 等 diagnostic signals。\n\n**证据链**:\n1. exception_only (99.83%) ≥ VOTA full (99.41%) — 压缩后的其他组件（loop, ae）不仅无益，反而引入 noise\n2. VOTA 跨模型 gap = 14.96pp — 因为 VOTA 包含 model-dependent 的 Tier 2 组件（loop compression 需要 execution reasoning）\n3. exception_only 在两个模型上均为最优组件 — isolate 出 Tier 1 后，相对优势 robust（虽然 DeepSeek 绝对值 seed-dependent）\n4. vota_no_ae (99.41%) = VOTA (99.41%) — actual/expected 移除无影响，exception 是真正的 active ingredient\n\n**论文中的表述**: \"VOTA's trace compression pipeline incidentally isolates exception events — the minimal sufficient diagnostic signal. Its value lies not in making traces shorter, but in separating high-specificity fault indicators from low-specificity behavioral patterns.\"\n\n**对 Method section 的影响**: VOTA 仍然完整描述（loop compression + exception + actual/expected），但 Results/Discussion 明确指出 exception isolation 是核心机制，其他组件是 model-capability-dependent bonus。\n\n## Story Spine\n\n- **Problem**: Existing trace SFT studies (DCSH, TracePile) evaluate only generative tasks and find limited, model-dependent benefit. No prior work has tested trace SFT on verdict tasks (binary pass/fail), nor identified what trace components drive classification performance.\n\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: DCSH (EMNLP Findings 2025) tested 5 trace formats on generation tasks, finding that \"no single trace representation consistently outperforms others.\" TracePile (NeurIPS 2025) scaled to 2.6M samples but only evaluated generation. TRACED (ICSE 2024) showed traces help encoder-based classification. All existing trace formats are designed for execution simulation, not fault detection.\n\n- **Our framing**: We reframe verdict prediction as **fault detection** and ask: what is the minimal sufficient diagnostic signal? Through systematic component ablation across two architecturally distinct models, we discover that exception events — compact, structured error summaries — are consistently the most effective trace component, achieving near-perfect accuracy on Qwen (99.83% ± 0.00, 3 seeds) and outperforming all alternatives on DeepSeek (91-99% vs VOTA 84.45%). Richer trace representations work only when the base model has sufficient execution reasoning capability, making them unreliable for general deployment.\n\n- **Headline result**: \"Exception events consistently achieve the highest verdict accuracy across models (Qwen 99.83%, DeepSeek 91-99%), outperforming VOTA traces (84.45% on DeepSeek, -14.96pp gap) — demonstrating that fault indicator isolation, not trace reasoning, is the robust mechanism for verdict prediction. DeepSeek seed sensitivity reveals LoRA training stability as a secondary factor for smaller models.\"\n\n## Story Arc\n\n1. **Setup**: Trace SFT for code tasks — promising but inconsistent in prior work (DCSH, TracePile). Unknown whether traces help verdict tasks and what trace design matters.\n\n2. **First result — traces help, compression helps more** (single model): no_trace 75.13% → full_trace 81.76% → VOTA 99.41%. Label-only loss ablation decomposes the gap: 26% gradient dilution, 74% information overload. Compression improves accuracy through higher information density, not just cost savings.\n\n3. **Twist — what component actually drives VOTA?** Component ablation reveals exception_only (99.83%) ≥ full VOTA (99.41%). The \"trace reasoning\" narrative is wrong — verdict prediction is fault detection. Tier 1 (exceptions + assertion-point) subsumes Tier 2 (loops). Actual/expected removal doesn't hurt (99.41%).\n\n4. **Cross-model stress test — consistent dominance, not universal perfection**: Exception_only is the best component on both models (even DeepSeek worst seed 91.09% > VOTA 84.45%), but absolute performance is seed-dependent on DeepSeek (91-99% vs Qwen's perfectly stable 99.83%). VOTA degrades by 14.96pp. The structurally simplest component is the most robust, but LoRA initialization sensitivity introduces variance on weaker models. Qwen seed robustness (3-seed variance = 0) + calibration (ECE=0.0027) 确认 exception signal 在强模型上的 reliability。DeepSeek seed sensitivity 揭示 training stability 作为 secondary bottleneck。\n\n5. **Design principle**: For verdict tasks, identify the highest-specificity diagnostic signal (exception events) and discard everything else. VOTA's value is fault indicator isolation — automatically extracting exceptions from noisy traces — not generic compression. Simpler signals are more robust; complexity tolerance depends on model capability.\n\n## Dataset Characteristics\n\n### Data duplication analysis\n\n| Condition | Total | Unique | Duplication rate | Cause |\n|-----------|-------|--------|------------------|-------|\n| exception_only_train | 4720 | 2858 | 39.45% | 同题多个 passing 解映射为相同 (input, output) — 无异常 → 同一 trace |\n| exception_only_test | 1190 | 607 | 48.99% | 同上 |\n| raw_traces | 5910 | 5803 | 1.81% | 完整 trace 含行号/变量值/内存地址，几乎不重复 |\n\n**Train-test input overlap: 0（无数据泄漏）**\n\n**解读**: exception_only 高重复率是 representation 的自然属性，非数据问题。多个正确解对同一测试用例执行时若均无异常，exception_only 表征退化为相同的空/无异常标记。这恰好说明 exception signal 的 compactness — 它仅编码 fault presence，不编码 solution identity。raw_traces 重复率极低 (1.81%) 因为完整 trace 包含 solution-specific 信息（变量值、内存地址）。尽管高重复率意味着有效训练样本少于名义数量，模型仍达 99.83% — 进一步确认 exception signal 的 learnability。\n\n## DeepSeek full_trace 处理\n\n**现状**: v1 坍塌（28.5%, 100% 预测 fail, 根因: 训练端 label 截断）。v2 重训启动但被 kill（Loop #6 ops worker 腾 GPU 给 vota_seed456）。v3 RUNNING（label-preserving truncation fix）。\n\n**处理方案**: v3 正在 cuda:0 上训练，预计 5/16 完成。\n\n**VOTA vs full_trace 截断机制区分** (D030 token analysis 确认):\n- **full_trace v1**: SFTTrainer right-truncation 切掉了 verdict **label** → 100% predict fail → 坍塌。根因: DeepSeek tokenizer 对 full_trace 产生 >2048 tokens, label 在截断窗口外。\n- **VOTA**: `to_chat_text()` 保留 verdict label（label 完整），但 91.7% 的样本 **trace content（input）被截断**（mean 2626 → ~2048 tokens）。84.45% 是真实结果，反映 trace 信息损失对模型学习的影响，**不是** label corruption。\n- **exception_only**: token 长度远低于 2048，不受截断影响，跨模型对比 clean。\n\n**对论文叙事的影响**: 论文中所有 \"right-truncation corrupts/removes the verdict label\" 措辞**对 VOTA 不成立**——label 完整保留，被截断的是 trace input。正确表述: \"trace content truncation causes information loss\"。VOTA cross-model gap (14.96pp) 有两个贡献因素: (1) model capability, (2) training-time trace truncation on DeepSeek (see D11)。\n\n## External Benchmark (DebugBench) Positioning\n\n**DebugBench 仅作泛化性验证，不作 trace importance 证据。**\n\n| Condition | Main Dataset | DebugBench | Δ interpretation |\n|-----------|-------------|------------|------------------|\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 96.71% | DebugBench 天花板高，baseline 已强 |\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 99.77% | +3.06pp lift — 边际 |\n| exception_only | 99.83% | 100.00% | +3.29pp lift — 边际 |\n\n**主数据集 trace lift = 24.70pp**（no_trace → exception_only），DebugBench trace lift 不到其 1/7。\n\n**论文定位规则**：\n- DebugBench → 泛化性证据（exception_only 100% = 跨数据集鲁棒性）\n- Trace importance 论证 → 仅依赖主数据集（24.7pp lift）\n- 禁止用 DebugBench 的 3.29pp lift 论证 \"trace 的核心价值\"——审稿人会攻击天花板效应\n\n## Venue Fit Risks\n\n| Risk | Assessment | Mitigation |\n|------|-----------|------------|\n| 单任务 | 仍然只在 test verdict prediction 上验证 | 跨模型验证部分弥补; Discussion 中讨论 generalization |\n| 单数据集 | HumanEval + MBPP-sanitized 是唯一数据源 (591 problems × 10 solutions) | 定位为 empirical study, 深度优于广度; 两个标准 benchmark 组合覆盖多样编程题型; exception_only 有效训练样本仅 2858 unique 即达 99.83%, 暗示 signal 的 simplicity 降低对数据量的依赖 |\n| \"只是 feature engineering\" | exception extraction 不复杂 | simplicity 本身是 contribution — 最简信号最 robust |\n| Negative result 味道 | VOTA 跨模型不 robust | Reframe: 识别 robust vs non-robust 组件边界是 positive |",

    }
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/artifacts/_project/report/adversarial_review_report.md",
```
```json
  "content": "# Adversarial Review Report: EMNLP 2026 Simulation\n\n**Paper**: \"Exception Events Are All You Need: Cross-Model Fault Detection for Verdict Prediction\"\n\n**Simulated venue**: EMNLP 2026 Main Conference (ARR May cycle)\n\n---\n\n## Reviewer 1: Methodology Expert\n\n**Overall Assessment: Borderline**\n**Confidence: 4/5**\n\n### Strengths\n\n1. **Clean experimental decomposition.** The information density decomposition (gradient dilution 26% vs. information overload 74%) via the `full_trace_label_only` ablation is a well-designed causal intervention. This is the most methodologically novel contribution — it separates training signal quality from input signal quality in a way that is reusable by future work.\n\n2. **Transparent reporting of negative/messy results.** The paper does not hide the DeepSeek seed 123 outlier, the VOTA truncation confound, or the DebugBench ceiling effect. The irreducible error analysis (GeneratorExit) is a model of thoroughness.\n\n### Weaknesses (by severity)\n\n**W1 (Major): Insufficient seed coverage — most conditions have n=1 seed.**\nThe headline robustness claim rests on 3 seeds for `exception_only` only. All other conditions (VOTA, full_trace, label_only, ae_only, loop_only, no_trace) report a single seed (42). This is a critical gap: the entire Claim 2 (gradient dilution decomposition) and Claim 3 (two-tier hierarchy) hang on single-seed point estimates. The 86.22% for `label_only` could shift substantially with different seeds, invalidating the 26/74 ratio. At minimum, `ae_only`, `label_only`, and `loop_only` need 3-seed validation on Qwen to confirm the hierarchy is stable.\n\n**Recommendation**: Run 3 seeds for `label_only` and `ae_only` at minimum. If infeasible, add explicit caveats that the decomposition ratio is a point estimate from a single seed.\n\n**W2 (Major): Statistical testing is incomplete for key comparisons.**\n- The `exception_only` vs. `ae_only` comparison (99.83% vs. 99.92%, p=0.80) correctly reports non-significance, but **no effect size or equivalence test** is provided. Non-significance ≠ equivalence. A TOST equivalence test with a meaningful margin (e.g., 1pp) would substantiate the \"statistically indistinguishable\" claim.\n- The 26/74 decomposition has no uncertainty quantification on the ratio itself. Bootstrap the ratio directly (gap_gradient / gap_total) to get a CI on the percentage split.\n- DeepSeek `exception_only` 3-seed results (99.33, 99.33, 91.09) — reporting σ=4.76pp from n=3 is essentially uninformative. The distribution is bimodal, not normal; σ is a misleading summary statistic. Acknowledge the bimodality explicitly and avoid parametric summaries.\n\n**W3 (Moderate): The VOTA cross-model gap is acknowledged as confounded but then used for narrative support.**\nThe paper correctly states \"the two factors cannot be disentangled\" (model capability vs. truncation), but then uses the 14.96pp gap repeatedly to argue that \"richer representations are model-dependent.\" This is overclaiming from confounded evidence. The group analysis (intact 100% vs. truncated 83.04%) provides *directional* evidence but cannot estimate the truncation contribution because the groups differ in other ways (e.g., problem complexity correlates with trace length). The honest framing is: \"VOTA degrades on DeepSeek; truncation is a plausible contributor, but the magnitude of its effect is unknown.\"\n\n**W4 (Moderate): No cross-condition comparison on DeepSeek for ablation.**\nThe two-tier hierarchy (Claim 3) is established entirely on Qwen. DeepSeek only has 4 conditions (exception_only, VOTA, full_trace, no_trace) — no `ae_only`, `loop_only`, or `label_only`. The hierarchy could look different on a weaker model. This limits the \"cross-model\" framing.\n\n**W5 (Minor): Confidence intervals assume independent Bernoulli trials despite acknowledged duplication.**\nThe paper notes 48.99% test set duplication for `exception_only` but reports CIs assuming n=1190. The deduplicated CI (n=607) is mentioned in passing but all main tables use n=1190. This inflates precision. At minimum, report the deduplicated CI as the primary one, or use cluster-robust CIs where duplicates form clusters.\n\n---\n\n## Reviewer 2: Domain Expert / Skeptic\n\n**Overall Assessment: Weak Reject**\n**Confidence: 4/5**\n\n### Strengths\n\n1. **Practical design principle is clear and actionable.** \"Extract the highest-specificity diagnostic signal and discard everything else\" is a useful takeaway for practitioners building trace-augmented systems. The paper demonstrates that over-engineering trace representations can hurt.\n\n2. **Honest self-assessment of contribution scope.** The paper explicitly acknowledges (Introduction, para 4) that exception dominance is \"not itself surprising\" and locates the novelty in the decomposition and cross-model analysis.\n\n### Weaknesses (by severity)\n\n**W1 (Major): The task may be trivially solvable, undermining the significance of findings.**\n99.83% accuracy with a simple presence/absence signal raises a fundamental question: **is this task interesting enough to warrant a paper?** The `exception_only` representation effectively reduces verdict prediction to: \"does the trace contain `[ERROR]`?\" → fail; \"does it not?\" → pass. This is a regex, not a learned representation. The paper argues that the *decomposition* of why other representations fail is the contribution, but if the task is solvable by string matching, the decomposition explains something obvious: noisy inputs hurt classifiers more than clean inputs.\n\nThe irreducible error analysis confirms this — the only errors are where the representation *incorrectly* marks a pass as containing errors (GeneratorExit). The model has learned precisely the trivial rule.\n\n**Counter-argument the paper should address**: What is the accuracy of a zero-shot regex baseline (`grep -c \"[ERROR]\"`)? If it matches 99.83%, the entire SFT pipeline is unnecessary and the contribution collapses. The paper never reports this baseline.\n\n**W2 (Major): Extreme narrowness of evaluation scope.**\n- **2 models, both ~7B, both instruction-tuned**: No encoder models (e.g., CodeBERT for classification), no larger models (13B+, 70B), no API models. The \"cross-model\" framing overstates the diversity — both are decoder-only instruction-tuned code LLMs from the same generation.\n- **1 primary dataset (591 problems)**: HumanEval + MBPP are toy benchmarks with short, self-contained functions. Real verdict prediction involves multi-file projects, complex dependencies, flaky tests, environment-dependent failures, timeout errors without exceptions, assertion failures that don't raise exceptions, etc. The exception_only signal would likely fail catastrophically on assertion-based test frameworks (e.g., pytest where `assert x == y` raises `AssertionError` for both logic errors and expectation mismatches).\n- **DebugBench validation is weak**: 96.71% no_trace baseline → only 3.29pp headroom. This does not validate trace importance; it shows the dataset is too easy.\n- **Python only**: No evidence this transfers to statically-typed languages where exception semantics differ (e.g., Java checked exceptions, Rust panics, Go error returns).\n\n**W3 (Major): The \"fault detection reframing\" is a known perspective, not a novel contribution.**\nThe SE community has long understood that test verdict prediction is fault detection. FuseFL (cited in the paper) already uses structured fault signals including error messages. TRACED uses execution-aware features for vulnerability detection (a fault detection task). The paper's reframing adds terminology but not conceptual novelty.\n\n**W4 (Moderate): Solution generation creates a distribution confound.**\nAll 5,910 solutions were generated by Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B — the same model used for evaluation. This means:\n1. The failure modes in the dataset are Qwen-specific (the types of bugs Qwen makes).\n2. The exception patterns are also Qwen-specific.\n3. Evaluating Qwen on its own failure modes may inflate accuracy (the model \"recognizes\" its own error patterns).\nThe paper acknowledges this in Limitations but dismisses it (\"does not affect verdict prediction\"). This dismissal is unjustified — a model may be better at recognizing bugs it typically makes than bugs from a different distribution.\n\n**W5 (Moderate): Missing practical motivation.**\nWhen would you need ML-based verdict prediction? If you can execute the code (which you must, to get the trace), you already have the verdict. The paper never addresses this deployment scenario question. Possible answers (predicting verdicts for unseen tests, or for partial executions) are not discussed.\n\n---\n\n## Reviewer 3: Writing & Presentation Expert\n\n**Overall Assessment: Weak Accept**\n**Confidence: 3/5**\n\n### Strengths\n\n1. **Exceptionally well-structured paper.** The progression from single-model ablation → decomposition → cross-model validation → cross-dataset validation is logical and easy to follow. Each section answers a clear question.\n\n2. **Tables and figures are information-dense.** Table 1 (cross-model) and Table 2 (ablation) convey the core results efficiently. The trace overview figure (Fig 1) provides helpful visual context. CIs are consistently reported.\n\n### Weaknesses (by severity)\n\n**W1 (Major): The title overclaims.**\n\"Exception Events Are All You Need\" implies universality across tasks and settings. The paper demonstrates this for one task (verdict prediction), one language (Python), two very similar models, and one dataset family. A more accurate title: \"Exception Events Suffice for Verdict Prediction: A Component Ablation Study.\" The AIAYN allusion sets expectations the paper cannot meet.\n\n**W2 (Moderate): Claim 1 framing is convoluted and defensive.**\nThe abstract contains a 4-line sentence describing the DeepSeek seed results with parenthetical qualifications. The Claim 1 statement in the Introduction is 5 lines long with inline statistics. This defensiveness — trying to simultaneously claim robustness while acknowledging the outlier — produces confusing prose. Suggestion: lead with the clean Qwen result as the primary finding, then dedicate a separate paragraph to DeepSeek seed sensitivity as a nuanced secondary finding. Don't try to fit both into a single sentence.\n\n**W3 (Moderate): Overclaiming in several specific places.**\n- Abstract: \"the first systematic component ablation of trace representations for verdict prediction\" — this is likely true but unfalsifiable unless the authors have done an exhaustive literature survey. Consider \"a systematic\" instead.\n- §5.1: \"trace representations should match the model capability floor, not the ceiling\" — this design principle is asserted from a 2-model comparison where the \"floor\" model has a known truncation confound. The evidence doesn't support such a general principle.\n- Conclusion: \"These results reframe verdict prediction as fault detection\" — the reframing is a perspective shift, not a result. The results are accuracy numbers; the reframing is interpretation.\n\n**W4 (Moderate): Related work positioning is incomplete.**\n- No mention of **test outcome prediction** literature from SE (e.g., Bertolino et al.'s work on test case prioritization, or ML-based test flakiness detection). These communities have studied pass/fail prediction for decades.\n- No comparison with **simple ML baselines** (logistic regression on trace features, random forest on exception presence/absence). The paper only compares LLM-based approaches against each other, missing the opportunity to show when LLMs are actually needed.\n- The TRACED citation discusses execution-aware *pretraining* for encoder models — a fundamentally different setup (encoder vs. decoder, pretraining vs. SFT). The comparison is not apples-to-apples but the paper doesn't acknowledge this.\n\n**W5 (Minor): DebugBench results are over-interpreted.**\nThe paper says \"the component ranking is preserved\" on DebugBench but all three conditions are within 3.29pp at >96% accuracy. At these margins, the ranking is not meaningfully testable — CIs likely overlap for VOTA vs. exception_only (99.77% vs. 100%, $\\Delta$=0.23pp). The paper should state that DebugBench confirms the method does not degrade, rather than claiming it \"preserves the ranking.\"\n\n**W6 (Minor): Appendix is heavy; some content belongs in the main paper.**\nThe irreducible error analysis (GeneratorExit) is one of the paper's strongest methodological contributions but is relegated to the appendix. The training dynamics section (loss/accuracy decoupling) is interesting but tangential. Consider promoting the irreducible error analysis and demoting the training dynamics.\n\n---\n\n## Meta-Review: Consolidated Analysis\n\n### Top Weaknesses Flagged by Multiple Reviewers\n\n| # | Weakness | R1 | R2 | R3 | Severity |\n|---|----------|----|----|-----|----------|\n| 1 | **Task triviality / missing regex baseline** | — | W1 | — | Critical |\n| 2 | **Narrow evaluation scope** (2 models, 1 dataset, Python only) | W4 | W2 | W1 | Critical |\n| 3 | **Single-seed conditions** for non-headline results | W1 | — | — | Major |\n| 4 | **VOTA confound used for narrative despite acknowledged limitations** | W3 | — | W3 | Major |\n| 5 | **Overclaiming** (title, design principles, reframing language) | — | W3 | W1,W3 | Major |\n| 6 | **Missing practical motivation** for the task | — | W5 | — | Moderate |\n\n### Most Likely Rejection Reason\n\n**\"The task is too simple and the evaluation too narrow to constitute a sufficient contribution for EMNLP Main.\"**\n\nA skeptical AC would synthesize R2-W1 (task triviality) and R2-W2 (narrow scope) into: the paper demonstrates that a binary classification task with an obvious feature (exception presence) is solvable by that feature, across two similar models on toy benchmarks. The decomposition (Claim 2) and hierarchy (Claim 3) are interesting but rely on single-seed estimates and apply only to this narrow setting. The cross-model story is weakened by the truncation confound.\n\nFindings would be a more appropriate venue unless the evaluation scope is expanded.\n\n### Priority Repair Order (for EMNLP Main acceptance)\n\n**P0 — Must fix before submission (9 days):**\n\n1. **Add a regex/heuristic baseline.** Implement `has_error = \"[ERROR]\" in trace` and report its accuracy. If it matches 99.83%, pivot the framing to: \"SFT is unnecessary for exception-based verdict prediction; the contribution is the decomposition showing *why* simpler signals dominate.\" If it doesn't match (e.g., because the model handles edge cases like GeneratorExit differently), this becomes a strength.\n\n2. **Run 3 seeds for `label_only` and `ae_only` on Qwen.** These are cheap experiments (~2h each × 2 extra seeds = ~8h total). Without them, Claims 2 and 3 rest on single-seed point estimates, which any methodological reviewer will flag.\n\n3. **Soften the title.** \"Exception Events Suffice for Verdict Prediction\" or \"Exception Events Are the Dominant Signal for Verdict Prediction: A Cross-Model Trace Ablation Study.\"\n\n**P1 — Should fix:**\n\n4. **Add a practical motivation paragraph** in the Introduction. Explain *when* verdict prediction without execution is useful (e.g., predicting test outcomes for candidate patches before execution to save CI compute; predicting flakiness; handling partial executions).\n\n5. **Tighten the VOTA cross-model narrative.** Remove all instances where the 14.96pp gap is used to argue general model-dependence of rich representations. Instead, frame it as: \"VOTA performance on DeepSeek is confounded by training-time truncation; the clean cross-model comparison is on exception_only (0.50pp gap).\"\n\n6. **Report deduplicated CIs as primary** for `exception_only` results, or use cluster-robust standard errors.\n\n7. **Promote irreducible error analysis** to the main paper (even 1 paragraph in Discussion). This is one of the paper's strongest selling points.\n\n**P2 — Nice to have:**\n\n8. **Add one more model** (e.g., CodeLlama-7B or StarCoder2-7B) for `exception_only` to strengthen the \"cross-model\" claim. A single condition on a third model is ~2h of compute.\n\n9. **Add one non-ML baseline** (logistic regression on binary exception-presence feature) to demonstrate that SFT adds value beyond the trivial feature.\n\n10. **Acknowledge test outcome prediction literature** from SE in Related Work.\n\n11. **Run DeepSeek ablation** for at least `ae_only` to test whether the two-tier hierarchy holds cross-model.\n\n---\n\n## Predicted Outcome\n\n| Reviewer | Score | Likely disposition |\n|----------|-------|--------------------|\n| R1 (Methodology) | Borderline | Leans reject without multi-seed fixes |\n| R2 (Domain/Skeptic) | Weak Reject | Needs scope expansion or framing pivot |\n| R3 (Writing) | Weak Accept | Fixable with title change and tightening |\n| **Meta** | **Borderline → likely Findings** | Main needs P0 fixes + ideally P1 |\n\n**Bottom line**: The paper has a clean experimental design and honest reporting, but the task triviality concern and narrow scope are the primary threats.
```
